r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
For a positive integer x, define f(x) as follows: "While x is even, keep dividing it by 2. The final value of x after these divisions is f(x)." For example, f(4)=f(2)=f(1)=1, and f(12)=f(6)=f(3)=3.
Given an integer sequence A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N) of length N, find \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N \sum_{j=i}^N f(A_i+A_j).

Input

The input is given in the following format from Standard Input:
N
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1\le N\le 2\times 10^5
- 1\le A_i\le 10^7
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2
4 8

Sample Output 1

5

f(A_1+A_1)=f(8)=1, f(A_1+A_2)=f(12)=3, f(A_2+A_2)=f(16)=1. Thus, Print 1+3+1=5.

Sample Input 2

3
51 44 63

Sample Output 2

384

Sample Input 3

8
577752 258461 183221 889769 278633 577212 392309 326001

Sample Output 3

20241214


Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

from collections import defaultdict
for k in range(K+1):
    M = 1 << (k+1)
    half = 1 << k
    mask = M - 1
    d = {}
    for a in A:
        r = a & mask  # since M power of two, a % M = a & (M-1)
        hi = a >> (k+1)
        if r in d:
           cnt, sum_hi = d[r]; d[r]=(cnt+1, sum_hi+hi)
        else: d[r]=(1, hi)
    for r,(cnt,sum_hi) in d.items():
        s = (half - r) & mask
        item = d.get(s)
        if item is None: continue
        cnt_s, sum_hi_s = item
        carry = 1 if r > half else 0
        if r == s:
           ans += 4*cnt*sum_hi + (2*carry + 1)*cnt*cnt
        else:
           ans += 2*(sum_hi*cnt_s + cnt*sum_hi_s) + (2*carry + 1)*cnt*cnt_s

Let's derive S from T carefully:
Original desired S = ∑_{1≤i≤j≤N} f(A_i+A_j).
Let T = ∑_{i=1}^N ∑_{j=1}^N f(A_i+A_j).
Then T = ∑_{i<j} 2 f(A_i+A_j) + ∑_{i} f(2A_i).
S = ∑_{i<j} f + ∑_{i} f(2A_i).
Since f(2A_i)=f(A_i), T = 2(S - ∑ f(A_i)) + ∑ f(A_i)? Wait S includes ∑_{i} f(2A_i) = ∑ f(A_i). Let D=∑ f(A_i). Then S = U + D where U=∑_{i<j} f. T = 2U + D. So T = 2(S-D)+D = 2S - D. Thus S = (T + D)/2. Yes.

Now, maybe there is a way to compute T using exact v2 contributions but we need include k=0..K. Let's prove formula for exact v2=k in more detail:
For pair (i,j), let k=v2(A_i+A_j). Then A_i+A_j = 2^k * m with m odd. Let M=2^{k+1}. Then (A_i+A_j) mod M = 2^k. Let r_i=A_i mod M, r_j=A_j mod M. Then (r_i+r_j) mod M = 2^k. Let high_i=A_i>>(k+1), high_j=A_j>>(k+1). Let carry = 1 if r_i+r_j ≥ M else 0. Then A_i+A_j = (high_i+high_j+carry)M + (r_i+r_j - carry M). The remainder is 2^k. Then f = (A_i+A_j)/2^k = 2(high_i+high_j+carry)+1. Good.

Counting:
For fixed k, we group by r. For each r, matching s = (2^k - r) mod M. For each i with r_i=r and j with r_j=s, condition holds. Summing over all r counts every ordered pair exactly once? Let's formalize:
Given ordered pair (i,j), let r=r_i. Then r_j must equal s=(2^k-r) mod M. In the loop over r, when key r is processed, it includes this pair. There is no other r' that includes this pair because r'=r_i fixed. So exactly once. Good. If r_i and r_j swapped, that's a different ordered pair and will be counted when processing r_j. Good.

Now, let's think about possible bug: In the contribution formula, `sum_hi` is sum of high parts for residue r. For ordered pairs from r to s, sum over i∈r, j∈s of 2*(hi_i+hi_j+carry)+1. Let's derive:
∑_{i,j} 2(hi_i+hi_j+carry)+1
= 2∑_{i,j} hi_i + 2∑_{i,j} hi_j + (2carry+1) cnt_r cnt_s.
First term: for each i, hi_i appears cnt_s times -> sum_hi_r * cnt_s.
Second term: for each j, hi_j appears cnt_r times -> cnt_r * sum_hi_s.
So formula = 2*(sum_hi_r*cnt_s + cnt_r*sum_hi_s) + (2carry+1)*cnt_r*cnt_s. Correct.

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    max_sum = 2 * max(A)
    K = max_sum.bit_length() - 1

    sum_f = 0
    for a in A:
        sum_f += a // (a & -a)

    total = 0
    for k in range(K + 1):
        half = 1 << k
        M = half << 1
        mask = M - 1
        shift = k + 1
        d = {}
        for a in A:
            r = a & mask
            hi = a >> shift
            item = d.get(r)
            if item is None:
                d[r] = (1, hi)
            else:
                d[r] = (item[0] + 1, item[1] + hi)
        for r, (cnt, sum_hi) in d.items():
            s = (half - r) & mask
            item_s = d.get(s)
            if item_s is None:
                continue
            cnt_s, sum_hi_s = item_s
            carry = 1 if r > half else 0
            total += 2 * (sum_hi * cnt_s + cnt * sum_hi_s) + (2 * carry + 1) * cnt * cnt_s

    ans = (total + sum_f) // 2
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

get = d.get
for a in A:
    r = a & mask
    hi = a >> shift
    item = get(r)
    if item is None:
        d[r] = (1, hi)
    else:
        d[r] = (item[0]+1, item[1]+hi)
But `get` bound to dict, after updates still works. However, if we use `get` local, it's slightly faster. But if we assign `d[r]`, the dict object mutates, bound method still valid. Good.
- In iteration, use `for r, item in d.items(): cnt, sum_hi = item` maybe avoid tuple unpack? Not significant.
- Use `total += 2 * (sum_hi * cnt_s + cnt * sum_hi_s) + (2 * carry + 1) * cnt * cnt_s`.

Could also store `d` values as two-element list and mutate to avoid tuple allocation? Tuple allocation is okay. But if we want faster, we can use list `[cnt, sum_hi]` and mutate:
item = d.get(r)
if item is None:
    d[r] = [1, hi]
else:
    item[0] += 1
    item[1] += hi
This avoids creating new tuple for each update, but creates list for new. Lists are mutable, but memory more. It may be faster? Tuple update creates new tuple each time; list update mutates. But list has overhead. Could be faster. However, using list values means iteration item_s is list, fine. But `d.get(r)` returns list, if item is None check. We can use lists. Let's consider:
d = {}
for a in A:
    r = a & mask
    hi = a >> shift
    item = d.get(r)
    if item is None:
        d[r] = [1, hi]
    else:
        item[0] += 1
        item[1] += hi
Then iteration:
for r, item in d.items():
    cnt = item[0]; sum_hi = item[1]
    s = ...
    item_s = d.get(s)
    if item_s is None: continue
    cnt_s = item_s[0]; sum_hi_s = item_s[1]
...
This avoids tuple allocation on update. But list creation for each new residue. Tuple creation for each new residue also. For updates, list mutates faster. But list values are mutable and can be accidentally modified? We don't modify during iteration. Good. Memory maybe slightly larger but okay. Which is better? In PyPy, list may be slower? Not sure. Tuple is immutable and small; updating tuple creates new tuple but tuple allocation is optimized. 5M tuple allocations maybe okay. List mutation might be faster but list has more memory. I'd stick with tuple for clarity. But if time limit strict, maybe list is better. Let's evaluate: 5M updates, tuple allocation 5M, okay. AtCoder PyPy can handle 5M tuple allocations easily. The inner loop over d.items also unpacks tuples. Fine.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1 + N]

    max_sum = 2 * max(A)
    K = max_sum.bit_length() - 1

    sum_f = 0
    for a in A:
        sum_f += a // (a & -a)

    total = 0

    for k in range(K + 1):
        half = 1 << k
        M = half << 1
        mask = M - 1
        shift = k + 1

        d = {}
        for a in A:
            r = a & mask
            hi = a >> shift
            item = d.get(r)
            if item is None:
                d[r] = (1, hi)
            else:
                d[r] = (item[0] + 1, item[1] + hi)

        for r, (cnt, sum_hi) in d.items():
            s = (half - r) & mask
            item_s = d.get(s)
            if item_s is None:
                continue

            cnt_s, sum_hi_s = item_s
            carry = 1 if r > half else 0

            total += 2 * (sum_hi * cnt_s + cnt * sum_hi_s) + (2 * carry + 1) * cnt * cnt_s

    ans = (total + sum_f) // 2
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
